Using Equations
49
,
47
and
Lemma
4
, and
arguing as in the proof of Lemma
12
, we get

\displaystyle L^{-4}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{x},x^{\prime},l^{\prime}_{y},y^{\prime}}\bar{q}^{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{YY^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-4}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{x},x^{\prime},l^{\prime}_{y},y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\Tr\left[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}(p^{XY}(x,y)\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-4}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{x},x^{\prime},l^{\prime}_{y},y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\Tr\left[(T^{M\rightarrow\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x},l^{\prime}_{y},\epsilon^{1/4}}(\rho^{M}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}))(p^{XY}(x,y)\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\Tr\left[\left(L^{-2}\sum_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}T^{M\rightarrow\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x},l^{\prime}_{y},\epsilon^{1/4}}(\rho^{M}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(p^{XY}(x,y)\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\Tr\left[\left(\left(L^{-2}\sum_{l^{\prime}_{x}l^{\prime}_{y}}T^{M\rightarrow\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x},l^{\prime}_{y},\epsilon^{1/4}}\left(\sum_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\rho^{M}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\right)\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\left.{}-\left(\sum_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\rho^{M}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\right)\right)(p^{XY}(x,y)\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y})\right]

\displaystyle{}+L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\Tr\left[\left(\sum_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}|X=x}(x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{Y^{\prime}|Y=y}(y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\rho^{M}_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}\right)(p^{XY}(x,y)\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\left\|\left(L^{-2}\sum_{l^{\prime}_{x}l^{\prime}_{y}}T^{M\rightarrow\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x},l^{\prime}_{y},\epsilon^{1/4}}(\rho()^{M}_{xy})\right)-\rho()^{M}_{xy}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot(p^{XY}(x,y)\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\|_{1})

\displaystyle{}+L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\Tr[\rho()^{M}_{xy}\,\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\epsilon^{1/8}L^{-5/2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\|\rho()^{M}_{xy}\|_{1}+(1+g(e))L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\Tr[\rho^{M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\epsilon^{1/8}L^{-1/2}\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\|\rho()^{M}_{xy}\|_{1}+(1+g(e))(1+\sqrt{\epsilon})L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\frac{\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}]}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\epsilon^{1/8}L^{-1/2}\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\|\rho()^{M}_{xy}\|_{1}+(1+g(e)+2\sqrt{\epsilon})L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\frac{\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\|_{1}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\epsilon^{1/8}L^{-1/2}\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\|\rho()^{M}_{xy}\|_{1}+\frac{(1+g(e)+2\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

The proof of the lemma is now complete.

\square

The reader may have noticed some differences between the statements of
Lemma
17
and Lemma
12
,
Lemma
15
and Lemma
10
,
Lemma
16
and Lemma
11
.
The notable looking differences arise from corrections due to
augmentation smoothing viz. from the application of
Lemma
4
. Part of the reason for these differences
is the presence of terms like
\rho(X)_{xy}
,
\rho(Y)_{xy}
etc.
in the non-pairwise independent case, which do not exist in the
pairwise independent case. The other part of the reason for the
differences is cosmetic: the proof of the fully quantum smooth convex
split lemma Lemma
7

uses flattening for
\alpha^{X}
and
\beta^{Y}
, whereas in the non pairwise
independent classical quantum covering lemma
Propostion
5
, we
do not need to flatten their classical analogues
q^{X}
and
q^{Y}
.
Nevertheless, an effect similar to flattening
\alpha^{X}
,
\beta^{Y}

persists in the statements of Lemmas
15
,

16
. Terms like
\frac{1}{q^{X}(x)}
,

\frac{1}{q^{Y}(y)}
in the statements of
Lemmas
15
,
16
are
related to flattening terms like
\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\alpha}}
,

\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\beta}}
in the statements of Lemmas
10
,

11
.

By Lemma
2
and Equation
50
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\left\|\sigma^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]-\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\right\|_{1}\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&(AB)^{-1}\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\left\|\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\rho^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}-{}\right.\right.\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\left.\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}\right]\right\|_{1}\\
&\leq&(AB)^{-1}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\left\|\rho^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}-\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}\right\|_{1}\right]\\
&=&L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\left\|\rho^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}-\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\right\|_{1}\\
&=&L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\left\|\rho^{\hat{M}}_{x,y}-\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\right\|_{1}\\
&=&\|\rho^{L_{X}XL_{Y}Y\hat{M}}-\hat{\rho}^{L_{X}XL_{Y}Y\hat{M}}\|_{1}\;\leq\;2\sqrt{2}\cdot\epsilon^{1/8}.\end{array}

(53)

Hence by triangle inequality and Equation
52
, in
order to prove
Proposition
5
, it suffices to show that

\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\leq 12\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{40\epsilon^{1/32}+8e+g(e)}.

(54)

By Corollary
2
and Equation
50
,
we have

\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\left\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(AB)^{-1}\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\left\|\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}-{}\right.\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\left.\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(AB)^{-1}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\left\|\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}-\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\left\|\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}-\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}p^{XY}(x,y)\left\|\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}-\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}\right\|_{1}\;=\;\|\hat{\rho}^{L_{X}XL_{Y}Y\hat{M}}-\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{L_{X}XL_{Y}Y\hat{M}}\|_{1}\;<\;25\cdot\epsilon^{1/8}.

Define

\mathrm{Bad}:=\{((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}):\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{1}>25\epsilon^{1/16}\}.

By Markov’s inequality,

\prob_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\mathrm{Bad}]<\epsilon^{1/16}.

For
((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)})\in\mathrm{Bad}
, we
define

\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}:=\hbox{\small l\kern-3.5pt\normalsize 0}
.
For
((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)})\not\in\mathrm{Bad}
, we
apply Lemma
1
to the states

\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}
,

\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}
to get a state

\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}
satisfying the
following properties:

\begin{array}[]{c}\forall((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}):\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}},\\
\forall((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)})\not\in\mathrm{Bad}:\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{1}<5\epsilon^{1/32},\\
\implies\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{1}\right]<5\epsilon^{1/32}+\epsilon^{1/16}<6\epsilon^{1/32},\\
\forall((l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}):\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+10\epsilon^{1/32})\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}].\end{array}

(55)

Hence by triangle inequality and Equations
54
,

55
, in order to prove
Proposition
5
, it suffices to show that

\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\leq 6\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{40\epsilon^{1/32}+8e+g(e)}.

(56)

Define
\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}
to be the orthogonal projector
from the ambient space
\hat{M}
onto
F^{\prime}_{\rho}
.
Using Equations
51
,
53
,

55
(some of these equations were stated
earlier for
\Tr\rho=1
but they continue to hold in the natural
fashion for general
\Tr\rho
too), we get

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle~~~\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\rho^{\hat{M}}]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}],\\
\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\,\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\geq&\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\,\sigma^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]-\|\sigma^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{1}\right.\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.{}-\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{1}\right]\\
&=&\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\Tr[\sigma^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]]\\
&&~~~~~~{}-\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\sigma^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}-\hat{\sigma}^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\\
&&~~~~~~{}-\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}-\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\\
&=&\Tr\rho^{\hat{M}}-\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\sigma^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}-\hat{\sigma}^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\\
&&~~~~~~{}-\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}-\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}(L_{X}X)^{A}(L_{Y}Y)^{B}\hat{M}}\|_{1}]\\
&\geq&(\Tr\rho^{\hat{M}})(1-2\sqrt{2}\cdot\epsilon^{1/8}-6\epsilon^{1/32})\;\geq\;(\Tr\rho^{\hat{M}})(1-9\epsilon^{1/32}).\end{array}

(57)

Using Proposition
1
and concavity of
square root, we see that

\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{1}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[2\sqrt{\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}]}\sqrt{\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}]-\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\rho^{\hat{M}}]}\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}+2\sqrt{\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}]}\sqrt{\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}]-\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\,\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.{}+\sqrt{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\cdot\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\Tr[\rho^{\hat{M}}]-\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\,\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]]}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~{}+\sqrt{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\cdot\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\cdot\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}]

From Equation
56
and convexity of the square
function, in order to prove
Proposition
5
, it suffices to show that

\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}^{2}]<\frac{40\epsilon^{1/32}+8e+g(e)}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

(58)

The left hand side of the above inequality is

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}-\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}^{2}]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\|\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\|_{2}^{2}]+\|\rho^{\hat{M}}\|_{2}\\
&&~~~{}-2\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\,\rho^{\hat{M}}]]\\
&\leq&(1+10\epsilon^{1/32})\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]\\
&&~~~~{}+(1+\sqrt{\epsilon})^{2}\left\|\frac{\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&&~~~~{}-2(1-\sqrt{\epsilon})\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr\left[\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left(\frac{\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\right)\right]\right]\\
&=&(1+10\epsilon^{1/32})\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]+\frac{(1+\sqrt{\epsilon})^{2}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\\
&&~~~~{}-\frac{2(1-\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{{}^{\prime}\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}]\right]\\
&\leq&(1+10\epsilon^{1/32})\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]+\frac{(1+\sqrt{\epsilon})^{2}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\\
&&~~~~{}-\frac{2(1-\sqrt{\epsilon})(1-4e-9\epsilon^{1/32})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\\
&\leq&(1+10\epsilon^{1/32})\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]\\
&&~~~~~~~~~{}+\frac{8e+23\epsilon^{1/32}-1}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\end{array}

(59)

where the first inequality follows from
Equations
55
,
47
, and the
second inequality follows from Equations
57
,

51
.

From Equations
58
and
59
,
in order to prove
Proposition
5
, it suffices to show the
following lemma.

Lemma 18

Suppose

\displaystyle\sqrt{L}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{8|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}{\epsilon^{3/8}}\cdot\max\left\{\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\frac{p^{X}(x)}{q^{X}(x)}\|\rho(X)^{M}_{xy}\|_{1},\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\frac{p^{Y}(y)}{q^{Y}(y)}\|\rho(Y)^{M}_{xy}\|_{1},\sum_{x,y}p^{XY}(x,y)\|\rho()^{M}_{xy}\|_{1}\right\},

and

\displaystyle\log A

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|q^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log f(e)+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log f(e)+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log A+\log B

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|q^{X}\otimes q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})+1+\log\epsilon^{-1/2}.

Then,

\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]<\frac{1+6\epsilon^{1/2}+g(e)}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

Given the lower bound on
\sqrt{L}
, we can write down the following
consequences of Lemmas
17
,

15
,
16
,
14
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+4\sqrt{e}+2\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|q^{X}\otimes q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\\
\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle L^{-3}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{y},y^{\prime}}q^{X}(x)\bar{q}^{YY^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\leq&\frac{4(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|q^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\\
\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle L^{-3}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{x},x^{\prime}}q^{Y}(y)\bar{q}^{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y)}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l_{y}y}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\leq&\frac{4(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\\
\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle L^{-4}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y,l^{\prime}_{x},x^{\prime},l^{\prime}_{y},y^{\prime}}\bar{q}^{XX^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})\bar{q}^{YY^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+g(e)+3\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.\end{array}

(60)

We have,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&(AB)^{-2}\cdot\sum_{a,\hat{a}=1}^{A}\sum_{b,\hat{b}=1}^{B}\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(\hat{a}),y(\hat{b}))}{q^{X}(x(\hat{a}))q^{Y}(y(\hat{b}))}\cdot\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\right.\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(\hat{a}),(l_{y}y)(\hat{b})}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}]\right]\\
&=&(AB)^{-2}\cdot\sum_{a,\hat{a}=1}^{A}\sum_{b,\hat{b}=1}^{B}\\
&&~~~~~~~\displaystyle{\E_{((l_{x}x)(\hat{a}),(l_{x}x)(a)),((l_{y}y)(\hat{b}),(l_{y}y)(b))}}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(\hat{a}),y(\hat{b}))}{q^{X}(x(\hat{a}))q^{Y}(y(\hat{b}))}\cdot\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\right.\\

(61)

We analyse the above summation by considering several cases.

,

.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{((l_{x}x)(\hat{a}),(l_{x}x)(a)),((l_{y}y)(\hat{b}),(l_{y}y)(b))}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(\hat{a}),y(\hat{b}))}{q^{X}(x(\hat{a}))q^{Y}(y(\hat{b}))}\cdot\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\right.$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\

&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}{q^{X}(x^{\prime})q^{Y}(y^{\prime})}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l^{\prime}_{x}x^{\prime}l^{\prime}_{y}y^{\prime}}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+g(e)+3\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\end{array}

where we used Equation

in the inequality above.

,

,

. We have similarly,

&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}]\right]\\
&=&L^{-2}\sum_{l_{x},x,l_{y},y}q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)\Tr\left[\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\left(\frac{p^{XY}(x,y)}{q^{X}(x)q^{Y}(y)}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{l_{x}xl_{y}y}\right)\right]\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+4\sqrt{e}+2\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|q^{X}\otimes q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|},\end{array}

in the inequality above.

AB
such terms.

From Equations
61
,

,

63
,

64
,
65
we get

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
&=&(AB)^{-2}\cdot\sum_{a,\hat{a}=1}^{A}\sum_{b,\hat{b}=1}^{B}\\
&&~~~~~~~\displaystyle{\E_{((l_{x}x)(\hat{a}),(l_{x}x)(a)),((l_{y}y)(\hat{b}),(l_{y}y)(b))}}\left[\frac{p^{XY}(x(\hat{a}),y(\hat{b}))}{q^{X}(x(\hat{a}))q^{Y}(y(\hat{b}))}\cdot\frac{p^{XY}(x(a),y(b))}{q^{X}(x(a))q^{Y}(y(b))}\right.\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(\hat{a}),(l_{y}y)(\hat{b})}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)(a),(l_{y}y)(b)}]\right]\\
&\leq&(AB)^{-2}\left(\frac{(A(A-1)B(B-1)(1+g(e)+3\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}+\frac{4AB(B-1)(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|q^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\right.\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}+\frac{4A(A-1)B(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.{}+\frac{AB(1+4\sqrt{e}+2\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|q^{X}\otimes q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\right)\\
&\leq&\frac{(1+g(e)+3\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}+\frac{4(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|q^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{A|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\\
&&{}+\frac{4(1+4\sqrt{e}+3\sqrt{\epsilon})f(e)2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{B|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}+\frac{(1+4\sqrt{e}+2\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|q^{X}\otimes q^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{AB|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.\end{array}

(66)

From the constraints on
\log A
,
\log B
, we get

\E_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\left[\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{M}}_{(l_{x}x)^{(A)},(l_{y}y)^{(B)}}]\right]\leq\frac{(1+g(e)+6\sqrt{\epsilon})}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

\square

Note that the entropic quantities in the statement of
Lemma
18
are
in terms of the original normalised probability distribution

p^{XY}
, and not in terms of
\hat{p}^{XY}
which was later renamed
to
p^{XY}
for notational convenience.
Since the dimension
L
of the augmenting Hilbert spaces
L_{X}
,
L_{Y}

is a free parameter in our proof, it can be chosen large enough as
in the requirement of Lemma
18
. Thus
Lemma
18
holds, which completes the proof of
Proposition
5
.

Arguing along similar lines, we can now prove a smooth multipartite
soft covering lemma without pairwise independence. This is the main
theorem of this paper.

Theorem 1

Let
k
be a positive integer. Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{k}
be
k
classical
alphabets. For any subset

S\subseteq[k]
, let
X_{S}:=(X_{s})_{s\in S}
.
Let
p^{X_{[k]}}
be a normalised probability distribution on
X_{[k]}
.
The notation
p^{X_{S}}
denotes the marginal distribution on
X_{S}
.
Let
q^{X_{1}},\ldots,q^{X_{k}}
be normalised
probability distributions on the respective alphabets.
For each
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in X_{[k]}
,
let
\rho^{M}_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}

be a subnormalised density matrix on
M
.
The classical quantum control state is now defined as

\rho^{X_{[k]}M}:=\sum_{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\in X_{[k]}}p^{X_{[k]}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})|x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\rangle\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}|^{X_{[k]}}\otimes\rho^{M}_{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}}.

Suppose
\mathrm{supp}(p^{X_{i}})\leq\mathrm{supp}(q^{X_{i}})
.
For any subset
S\subseteq[k]
, let

q^{X_{S}}:=\times_{s\in S}q^{X_{s}}
.
Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{k}
be positive integers.
For each
i\in[k]
, let

x_{i}^{(A_{i})}:=(x_{i}(1),\ldots,x_{i}(A_{i}))
denote a
|A_{i}|
-tuple
of elements from
X_{i}
.
Denote the
A_{i}
-fold product alphabet

X_{i}^{A_{i}}:=X_{i}^{\times A_{i}}
, and the product probability distribution

q^{X_{i}^{A_{i}}}:=(q^{X_{i}})^{\times A_{i}}.

For any collection of tuples

x_{i}^{(A_{i})}\in X_{i}^{A_{i}}
,
i\in[k]
, we
define the sample average covering state

\sigma^{M}_{x_{1}^{(A_{1}},\ldots,x_{k}^{(A_{k})}}:=(A_{1}\cdots A_{k})^{-1}\sum_{a_{1}=1}^{A_{1}}\cdots\sum_{a_{k}=1}^{A_{k}}\frac{p^{X_{[k]}}(x_{1}(a_{1}),\ldots,x_{k}(a_{k}))}{q^{X_{1}}(x_{1}(a_{1}))\cdots q^{X_{k}}(x_{k}(a_{k}))}\rho^{M}_{x_{1}(a_{1}),\ldots,x_{k}(a_{k})},

where the fraction term above represents the ‘change of measure’ from
the product probability distribution
q^{X_{[k]}}
to the
joint probability distribution
p^{X_{[k]}}
.

Let the covering be done using independent choices of tuples

x_{1}^{(A_{1})},\ldots,x_{k}^{(A_{k})}
from the distributions

\bar{q}^{X_{1}^{A_{1}}},\ldots,\bar{q}^{X_{k}^{A_{k}}}
, which satisfy
the consequences of loss of pairwise independence,
akin to the situation in Proposition
5
, up
to scale factors of

(e,f(e),g(e))
. Suppose for each non-empty subset

\{\}\neq S\subseteq[k]
,

\sum_{s\in S}\log A_{s}>D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{X_{S}M}\|q^{X_{S}}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log f(e)+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2}.

Then,

\E_{x_{1}^{(A_{1})},\ldots,x_{k}^{(A_{k})}}\left[\left\|\sigma^{M}_{x_{1}^{(A_{1})},\ldots,x_{k}^{(A_{k})}}-\rho^{M}\right\|_{1}\right]<(2\sqrt{10\cdot 2^{k}\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{30\cdot 2^{2k}\epsilon^{1/32}+2^{k+1}e+g(e)}}+2^{k}e)(\Tr\rho).

Remarks:

1. The scale factor of
f(e)
in the consequences of loss of pairwise
independence in the statement of Theorem
1
means that
for any sequence of coordinates
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})
,

a_{i}\in[A_{i}]
, for any sequence of symbols
(x_{a_{1}},\ldots,x_{a_{k}})
,
for any non-empty subset
\{\}\neq S\subset[k]
, for any sequence
of coordinates
(a^{\prime}_{s})_{s\in S}
with
a_{s}\neq a^{\prime}_{s}
for all
s\in S
,

\E_{(x^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S}|(x_{a_{s}})_{s\in S}}[\rho^{M}_{(x_{a_{t}})_{t\in\bar{S}},\,(x^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{s}})_{s\in S}}]=:\rho(\bar{S})^{M}_{x_{a_{1}},\ldots,x_{a_{k}}}\leq f(e)\rho^{M}_{(x_{a_{t}})_{t\in\bar{S}}},

where the expectation is taken over independent choices from the
distributions
\bar{q}^{X^{\prime}_{s}|X_{s}=x_{a_{s}}}
,
s\in S
, and

\rho^{M}_{(x_{a_{t}})_{t\in\bar{S}}}
is the quantum state of register

M
when the symbols
(x_{a_{t}})_{t\in\bar{S}}
are fixed and
the symbols in
(a_{s})_{s\in S}
are averaged over according to independent
choices from the distribution
q^{X_{S}}
.

2. A factor of
k
comes in the error bound on the right hand side
of Theorem
1

due to using Lemma
2
for
k
orthogonal tilting
directions that arise from the
k
covering parties. However it is
subsumed by factors of
2^{k}
that are arise from the
k
party
analogue of Lemma
3
. The factor of

2^{k+1}e
in the error bound arises from the
k
party analogue
of Equation
36
, where we now have to do

2^{k}-1
intersections of classical probability distributions.

Similarly, we can also show the following smooth version of CMG
covering without pairwise independence.

Lemma 19

Consider the CMG covering problem of Section
2
,
where there is a loss of pairwise
independence in Alice’s obfuscation strategy when she chooses

L
symbols
x_{l^{\prime},1},\ldots,x_{l^{\prime},L}\in\mathcal{X}
conditioned on a sample

x^{\prime}_{l^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, and a similar loss of pairwise independence in
Bob’s obfuscation strategy. Suppose these are the only two places of
loss of pairwise independence, with the consequences of the loss holding
up to scale factors of
(e,f(e),0)
. Suppose

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}+\log L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}X:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log M^{\prime}+\log M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}+\log M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}+\log M^{\prime}+\log M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}+\log M^{\prime}+\log L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\log L^{\prime}+\log M^{\prime}+\log L+\log M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1/2}.

Then,

\mathrm{CMG}<2\sqrt{160\cdot\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{750\epsilon^{1/32}+8e}}+4e

6
Inner bound for the wiretap interference channel

In a wiretap quantum interference channel, there are two senders Alice
and Bob and their corresponding intended receivers Charlie and Damru.
There is a third receiver Eve, corresponding to an eavesdropper.
Alice would like to
send a classical message
m_{1}\in[2^{R_{1}}]
to Charlie. Similarly,
Bob would like to send
m_{2}\in[2^{R_{2}}]
to Damru.
Charlie and Damru should be able to decode correctly with large
probability at their
respective channel outputs and recover
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
.
It is okay
if Damru gets some information about Alice’s message
m_{1}
in addition to
the message
m_{2}
intended for him; similarly for Charlie. However,
Eve should have at most negligible information about any message
tuple
(m_{1},m_{2})
.

The parties have at their disposal
a quantum channel
\mathcal{N}:AB\rightarrow CDE
with input
Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
and output Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{E}
.
Though
\mathcal{N}
has quantum inputs and quantum
outputs, we will assume without loss of generality that the inputs are
classical, because in the inner bound below, there is a standard
optimisation
step over a choice of all ensembles of input states to Alice and Bob.
In other words, we fix classical to quantum encodings

x\mapsto\rho^{A}_{x}
,
y\mapsto\rho^{B}_{y}
.
Thus, we will henceforth think of
\mathcal{N}
as a classical quantum (cq)
channel with its two classical input alphabets being denoted by

\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}
, and the quantum output alphabets by
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathcal{D}
,
\mathcal{E}
.

We now describe the strategy of Chong, Motani, Garg, El Gamal

[
CMGE08
]
, adapted to the one shot quantum case by

[
Sen21a
]
, to achieve a rate inner bound for
sending
(m_{1},m_{2})
over
\mathcal{N}
when there is no eavesdropper Eve.
As shown below, we will suitably modify that strategy to ensure privacy
against Eve. For brevity, we will call the overall coding strategy
as the CMG wiretap strategy. For
this, we need to define additional classical alphabets
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
,
another ‘timesharing’ classical alphabet
\mathcal{Q}
,
a ‘control probobablity distribution’

p(q,x^{\prime},x,y^{\prime},y)=p(q)p(x^{\prime},x)p(y^{\prime},y),

(67)

and a ‘control classical quantum state’

\sigma^{QX^{\prime}XY^{\prime}YCDE}:=\sum_{q,x^{\prime},x,y^{\prime},y}p(q)p(x^{\prime},x)p(y^{\prime},y)|q,x^{\prime},x,y^{\prime},y\rangle\langle q,x^{\prime},x,y^{\prime},y|^{QX^{\prime}XY^{\prime}Y}\otimes\sigma^{CDE}_{x^{\prime}xy^{\prime}y},

(68)

where
\sigma^{CDE}_{x^{\prime}xy^{\prime}y}:=\sigma^{CDE}_{xy}
similar to the
definitions in Section
2
.

The CMG wiretap strategy uses
rate splitting
for both Alice and
Bob. By this we mean that Alice ‘splits’ her message
m_{1}\in[2^{R_{1}}]

into a pair
(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1})\in[2^{R^{\prime}_{1}}]\times[2^{R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}}]
.
Similarly Bob ‘splits’ his message
m_{2}\in[2^{R_{2}}]

into a pair
(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1})\in[2^{R^{\prime}_{2}}]\times[2^{R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}}]
.
The intention is that
m^{\prime}_{1}
,
m^{\prime}_{2}
should be decodable by both
Charlie and Damru. The message
m^{\prime\prime}_{1}
, which should of course be
decodable by Charlie, need not satisfy any requirement with respect
to Damru; similarly for message
m^{\prime\prime}_{2}
. Of course, the state of
Eve for any quadruple
(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1},m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime\prime}_{2})
should be almost
independent of the quadruple for the privacy requirement to be met.
The intuition, though not the actual decoding strategy, is that
the
m^{\prime}_{1}
,
m^{\prime}_{2}
parts of the encoded messages
are ‘easy’ to decode at both Charlie and Damru. So intuitively
Charlie can decode
m^{\prime}_{2}
and use it as side information in order to
decode
m^{\prime\prime}_{1}
; similarly for Damru. It is known that this strategy
of rate splitting enhances the achievable inner bound in many important
examples of interference channels.
The quantities
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
can be optimised in a later step in order
to get the largest inner bound possible.

We now describe the codebook, Alice and Bob’s encoding and obfuscation,
and Charlie and Damru’s decoding strategies.

Codebook:

Let
0\leq\epsilon\leq 1
.
Let
L^{\prime}
,
L
,
M^{\prime}
,
M
be positive integers.
As will become clear very soon, these four integers play the obfuscation
role as described for the CMG covering problem in
Section
2
.
First generate a sample
q
from the distribution
p^{Q}
.
For each message

m^{\prime}_{1}\in[2^{R^{\prime}_{1}}]
independently generate samples

x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},1),\ldots,x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime})
from the distribution
p^{X^{\prime}|Q=q}
.
Similarly, for each message

m^{\prime}_{2}\in[2^{R^{\prime}_{2}}]
independently generate samples

y^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{2},1),\ldots y^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{2},M^{\prime})
from the distribution

p^{Y^{\prime}|Q=q}
.
Now for each codeword
x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime})
,
l^{\prime}\in[L^{\prime}]

independently generate samples

x(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)
from the distribution

p^{X|X^{\prime}=x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime}),Q=q}
for all

(m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)\in[2^{R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}}]\times[L]
.
Similarly for each codeword
y^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime})
,
m^{\prime}\in[M^{\prime}]
,
independently generate samples

y(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{2},m)
from the probability distribution

p^{Y|Y^{\prime}=y^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime})Q=q}
for all

(m^{\prime\prime}_{2},m)\in[2^{R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}}]\times[M]
. These samples together
consititute the random codebook. We augment the random codebook
as in
[
Sen21a
]
to get a random augmented codebook.
This random augmented codebook is revealed to Alice, Bob, Charlie,
Damru and Eve.

Encoding and obfuscation:

To send message
m_{1}=(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1})
, Alice picks up a symbol

x(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)

for a uniformly random pair
(l^{\prime},l)\in[L^{\prime}]\times[L]

from the codebook and inputs the
state
\rho_{x(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)}^{A}
into the input
\mathcal{A}
of
the channel
\mathcal{N}
.
Similarly, to send message
m_{2}=(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime\prime}_{2})
, Bob picks up
the symbol
y(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{2},m)

for a uniformly random pair
(m^{\prime},m)\in[M^{\prime}]\times[M]

from the codebook and inputs the
state
\rho_{y(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{2},m)}^{B}
into the input
\mathcal{B}
of

\mathcal{N}
.

Decoding:

Charlie decodes the tuple
m_{1}=(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1})
using

simultaneous non-unique decoding
as explained in

[
Sen21a
]
, obtaining his guess
\hat{m}_{1}
for
m_{1}
.
Damru decodes the tuple
m_{2}=(m^{\prime}_{2},m^{\prime\prime}_{2})
using

simultaneous non-unique decoding
,
obtaining his guess
\hat{m}_{2}
for
m_{2}
. By simultaneous decoding,
we mean that Charlie’s decoded pair
(\hat{m^{\prime}}_{1},\hat{m^{\prime\prime}}_{1})
is obtained
by applying a single POVM element to the state at the channel output

\mathcal{C}
; this POVM element is not composed from ‘simpler’ POVM elements
like e.g. the successive cancellation decoder which first decodes,
say
\hat{m^{\prime}}_{1}
, and then uses it as side information to decode

\hat{m^{\prime\prime}}_{1}
. By non-unique
decoding, we mean that Charlie’s decoding POVM element for a fixed
tuple
(\hat{m^{\prime}}_{1},\hat{m^{\prime\prime}}_{1})
can be thought of as a ‘union’,
ranging over
(l^{\prime},l,m^{\prime}_{2})\in[L^{\prime}]\times[L]\times[2^{R^{\prime}_{2}}]
, of
projectors for each possible tuple

(\hat{m^{\prime}}_{1},l^{\prime},\hat{m^{\prime\prime}}_{1},l,m^{\prime}_{2})
. The ‘actual or unique
identity’ of

(l^{\prime},l,m^{\prime}_{2})
in the ‘union’ is immaterial as long as the correct

(\hat{m^{\prime}}_{1},\hat{m^{\prime\prime}}_{1})=(m^{\prime}_{1},m^{\prime\prime}_{1})
is decoded.

Correctness:

We want that

\prob[(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})]\leq\epsilon,

where the probability is over the uniform choice of the message pair

(m_{1},m_{2})\in[2^{R_{1}}]\times[2^{R_{2}}]
, and actions of the
encoder, channel and decoders.

Privacy:

We want that

\E_{(m_{1},m_{2})}[\|\sigma^{E}_{m_{1},m_{2}}-\sigma^{E}\|_{1}]\leq\epsilon,

where the expectation is over the uniform choice of the message pair

(m_{1},m_{2})\in[2^{R_{1}}]\times[2^{R_{2}}]
,

\sigma^{E}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
denotes the quantum state of Eve when message
pair
(m_{1},m_{2})
is transmitted, and
\sigma^{E}
is the marginal of
the control state on
E
from Equation
68
.
Note that
\sigma^{E}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
can be defined from the control state

\sigma^{QX^{\prime}XY^{\prime}YE}
given the codebook.

If there exist such coding
schemes for a particular channel
\mathcal{N}
, we say that there exists
an
(R_{1},R_{2},\epsilon)
-wiretap quantum interference channel code

for sending private classical information through
\mathcal{N}
.

We are interested in the variant of the codebook construction where
pairwise independence is lost in the following step of constructing
Alice’s part of the codebook.

Now for each codeword
x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime})
,
l^{\prime}\in[L^{\prime}]

independently generate samples

x(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime},m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)
from the distribution

p^{X|X^{\prime}=x^{\prime}(m^{\prime}_{1},l^{\prime}),Q=q}
for all

(m^{\prime\prime}_{1},l)\in[2^{R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}}]\times[L]
.

A similar loss of pairwise independence occurs in the corresponding
step of constructing Bob’s part of the codebook. There is no further
loss of pairwise independence. The consequences of
the loss of pairwise independence hold up to scale factors of

(e,f(e),0)
. Loss of pairwise independence in these parts of
codebook construction makes computational sense as these parts
require the largest amount of random bits because they contain
most of the codeword samples.

It turns out that the simultaneous non-unique decoding strategy
for Charlie and Damru without any privacy considerations,
already described in
[
Sen21a
]
,
continues to work with a natural slight degradation of parameters
when pairwise
independence is lost up to scale factors of
(e,f(e),0)
. This
is because the strategy is based on the same machinery of
tilting and augmentation smoothing. The requirement of privacy against
Eve comes from the CMG covering problem. We have already shown a
smooth inner bound for this problem under loss of pairwise independence
in Lemma
19
. Combining both simultaneous
non-unique decoding for Charlie, Damru together with the CMG covering
obfuscation against Eve, we get the following inner bound for sending
private classical information over a quantum interference channel.

Theorem 2

Consider the quantum interference channel with classical quantum
control state
\sigma^{QX^{\prime}XY^{\prime}YCDE}
defined in
Equation
68
.
Let
0<\epsilon<1
.
Consider a rate octuple

(R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime},R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1},L,R^{\prime}_{2},M^{\prime},R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2},M)
satisfying
the following inequalities:

\displaystyle R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}+L

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(X:C|X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{1}+L^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(X:C|Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}+L+R^{\prime}_{2}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(XY^{\prime}:C|X^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{1}+L^{\prime}+L+R^{\prime}_{2}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(XY^{\prime}:C|Q)_{\sigma}-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}+M

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(Y:D|X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{2}+M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(Y:D|X^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}+M+R^{\prime}_{1}+L^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(YX^{\prime}:D|Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle R_{2}+M^{\prime}+M+R^{\prime}_{1}+L^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I_{H}^{\epsilon}(YX^{\prime}:D|Q)_{\sigma}-2-\log\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle L^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}X:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log f(e)+\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+L+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I_{\infty}^{\epsilon}(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+3+\log\epsilon^{-1}.

Then there exists
an
(R_{1},R_{2},\alpha)
-wiretap quantum interference channel code
for sending private classical information through
\mathcal{N}
where

\alpha<2^{2^{14}}\epsilon^{1/6}+2\sqrt{160\cdot\epsilon^{1/64}+\sqrt{750\epsilon^{1/32}+8e}}+4e.

Standard Fourier-Motzkin
elimination of
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
,
L^{\prime}
,
L
,
M^{\prime}
,
M
can now be used
to obtain an inner bound in terms of only
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
.

The above one shot theorem automatically gives us the following
corollary in the asymptotic iid limit.

Corollary 3

Consider the setting of Theorem
2
.
Consider an octuple of rate per channel use

(R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime},R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1},L,R^{\prime}_{2},M^{\prime},R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2},M)
satisfying
the following inequalities:

\displaystyle R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}+L

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(X:C|X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)

\displaystyle R_{1}+L^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(X:C|Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle R_{1}-R^{\prime}_{1}+L+R^{\prime}_{2}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(XY^{\prime}:C|X^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)

\displaystyle R_{1}+L^{\prime}+L+R^{\prime}_{2}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(XY^{\prime}:C|Q)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}+M

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(Y:D|X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)

\displaystyle R_{2}+M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(Y:D|X^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle R_{2}-R^{\prime}_{2}+M+R^{\prime}_{1}+L^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(YX^{\prime}:D|Y^{\prime}Q)_{\sigma}-\log f(e)

\displaystyle R_{2}+M^{\prime}+M+R^{\prime}_{1}+L^{\prime}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle I(YX^{\prime}:D|Q)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle L^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}X:E)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+\log f(e)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}+\log f(e)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+L

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}:E)_{\sigma}+\log f(e)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}+M^{\prime}+L+M

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle I(X^{\prime}XY^{\prime}Y:E)_{\sigma}.

Then there exists
a per channel use rate
(R_{1},R_{2})
-wiretap quantum interference
channel code
for sending private classical information through
\mathcal{N}
with
asymptotically vanishing decoding and privacy error.
Standard Fourier-Motzkin
elimination of
R^{\prime}_{1}
,
R^{\prime}_{2}
,
L^{\prime}
,
L
,
M^{\prime}
,
M
can now be used
to obtain an inner bound in terms of only
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
.

We note that the above corollary with loss of pairwise independence
was unknown earlier even in the classical asymptotic iid setting.

7
Simpler proof of smooth multipartite convex split

In this section, we give a simpler proof of
Lemma
7
which only tilts and augments
the registers
X
,
Y
in the state
\sigma^{X^{A}Y^{B}M}
. The
augmentation only doubles the dimensions of
X
,
Y
. The register
M

is not affected. The advantage of the simpler style of proof is that
it can be extended to prove
our variant of the fully smooth decoupling theorem in the next section.
The simpler proof style is somewhat dual to the more involved proof in
Section
4
which tilted and augmented register

M
but left registers
X
,
Y
unchanged. The advantage of the
more involved proof was that it could be extended to
prove the smooth multipartite soft covering
lemma without pairwise independence in
Section
5
.

As in Section
4
, we assume without loss of
generality that the density matrix
\rho^{XYM}
is normalised. We
flatten by applying the superoperators

\mathcal{F}_{\alpha}^{X\rightarrow L_{X}X}
,
\mathcal{F}_{\beta}^{Y\rightarrow L_{Y}Y}
,

\mathcal{F}_{\rho}^{M\rightarrow L_{M}M}
to
\rho^{XYM}
, followed by
renaming
X\equiv L_{X}X
,
Y\equiv L_{Y}Y
and

M\equiv L_{M}M
as in Section
4
. Thus,
we get orthogonal projectors

\Pi^{XYM}(3)
,
\Pi^{XM}(1)
and
\Pi^{YM}(2)
satisfying
Equation
16
as in Section
4
.
To finish the proof of Lemma
7
, we
only need to show the validity of Equation
17

of Section
4
.

We will now need to augment and tilt the Hilbert spaces
X
,
Y
. We
will not touch
M
. This is the place from where the present proof
starts differing from the proof in Section
4
.
Define new Hilbert spaces
\bar{X}
,
\bar{Y}
isomorphic to
X
,
Y
.
Define the natural isometric bijections

|x\rangle^{X}\mapsto|x\rangle^{\bar{X}}
,

|y\rangle^{Y}\mapsto|y\rangle^{\bar{Y}}
.
Define the augmented Hilbert spaces
\hat{X}:=X\oplus\bar{X}
,

\hat{Y}:=Y\oplus\bar{Y}
. Let
0<\epsilon<1
.
Define the tilting map

T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}:|x\rangle^{X}\mapsto\sqrt{1-\epsilon^{1/4}}|x\rangle^{X}+\epsilon^{1/8}|x\rangle^{\bar{X}}.

Define
T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}
similarly.
Define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}&\equiv&\rho^{XYM},\\
\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}&:=&(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}\otimes T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}),\\
\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}&:=&\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\\
&&{}-\spanning\{((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}},\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{YM}-\Pi(2)^{YM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}},\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}\otimes T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})\},\\
\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}&:=&\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\circ\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M},\\
\alpha^{\hat{X}}&:=&\alpha^{X},~~~\beta^{\hat{Y}}:=\beta^{Y},\\
\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}&\equiv&\tau^{X^{A}Y^{B}M}:=\alpha^{X^{A}}\otimes\beta^{Y^{B}}\otimes\rho^{M},\\
\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}&\equiv&\sigma^{X^{A}Y^{B}M}:=\frac{1}{AB}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\rho^{X_{a}Y_{b}M}\otimes\alpha^{X^{-a}}\otimes\beta^{Y^{-b}}\\
&\equiv&\frac{1}{AB}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\rho^{\hat{X}_{a}\hat{Y}_{b}M}\otimes\alpha^{\hat{X}^{-a}}\otimes\beta^{\hat{Y}^{-b}},\\
\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}&:=&\frac{1}{AB}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}_{a}\hat{Y}_{b}M}\otimes\alpha^{\hat{X}^{-a}}\otimes\beta^{\hat{Y}^{-b}},\\
\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}&:=&\frac{1}{AB}\sum_{a=1}^{A}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}_{a}\hat{Y}_{b}M}\otimes\alpha^{\hat{X}^{-a}}\otimes\beta^{\hat{Y}^{-b}}.\end{array}

(69)

The following properties can be proved easily as in
Section
4
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}M}&=&(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}),~~\hat{\rho}^{\hat{Y}M}=(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{YM}),\\
\|\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\|_{1}&\leq&4\sqrt{2}\epsilon^{1/8},\\
\Tr[\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]&\geq&1-121\epsilon^{1/4},\\
\implies\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\|_{1}&\leq&22\epsilon^{1/8},~~\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\|_{1}\leq 28\epsilon^{1/8},\\
\implies\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}&\leq&4\sqrt{2}\epsilon^{1/8},~~\|\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}\leq 28\epsilon^{1/8}.\end{array}

(70)

By Lemma
1
, there exists a subnormalised density
matrix
\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}
such that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}&\leq&\sqrt{28}\epsilon^{1/16},\\
\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}&\leq&\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M},\\
\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}^{2}&\leq&(1+11\epsilon^{1/16})\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}],\\
\implies\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}&<&9\epsilon^{1/16}.\end{array}

(71)

From Equation
17
and the above calculation, it
suffices to show

\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}<11\epsilon^{1/32}.

Define
\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}
to be the projector from

\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M
onto

\mathrm{supp}(\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M})=F_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\otimes A}\otimes F_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}\otimes B}\otimes F^{\prime}_{\rho}.

Then,

\Tr[\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]\geq\Tr[\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]-\frac{1}{2}\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{1}\geq 1-5\epsilon^{1/16},

(72)

as
\Tr[\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]=1
because

\mathrm{supp}(\sigma^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M})\leq\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}.

By Proposition
1
, it thus suffices to show

\sqrt{\Tr[\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]}\cdot\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}<11\epsilon^{1/32}-5\epsilon^{1/32}=6\epsilon^{1/32},

which implies that it suffices to show,

\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}^{2}<\frac{36\epsilon^{1/16}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

(73)

Using Equations
71
,
72
,

16
, the left
hand side of the above equation can be simplified as

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}-\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}^{2}-2\Tr[\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]+\|\tau^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+11\epsilon^{1/16})\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]-\frac{2(1-\sqrt{\epsilon})\Tr[\sigma^{{}^{\prime}\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\Pi^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}+\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle(1+11\epsilon^{1/16})\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]-\frac{2(1-\sqrt{\epsilon})(1-5\epsilon^{1/16})}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}+\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle(1+11\epsilon^{1/16})\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]-\frac{1-13\epsilon^{1/16}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

From the above and Equation
73
, it thus
suffices to show

\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]<\frac{1+\epsilon^{1/16}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

(74)

We now prove four lemmas which are crucially required to prove
Equation
74
. They are the analogues of
Lemmas
9
,
10
,
11
,

12
in Section
4
.

Lemma 20

\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]\leq\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\beta^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}||F^{\prime}_{\beta}||F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

Using Equations
16
,
69
,

70
, we get

\displaystyle\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}))(\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\circ\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})))\circ(\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\circ\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}))\circ((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM})))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})))\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}\circ(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM}))))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})))\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(3)^{XYM}))\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(3)^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~{}\circ((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XYM})))\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(3)^{XYM}\circ\rho^{XYM}))\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(3)^{XYM}\circ\rho^{XYM})\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\Pi(3)^{XYM}\circ\rho^{XYM}\|_{\infty}\cdot\Tr[\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]\;\leq\;\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\beta^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}||F^{\prime}_{\beta}||F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Above, we used the fact that

\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})\geq\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}

in the third equality,

\displaystyle((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}))

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~{}\circ(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM})-(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XYM}-\Pi(3)^{XYM})

in the sixth equality,

T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes T^{Y\rightarrow\hat{Y}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M}

is an isometry in the tenth equality.
The proof of the lemma is now complete.

\square

Lemma 21

\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}M}]\leq\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}||F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

Using Equations
16
,
69
,

70
, we get

\displaystyle\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}M}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(\hat{\rho}^{\hat{X}M}\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}\hat{M}}]\;=\;\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})(\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\circ\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\circ(\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\circ\rho^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}))\circ((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM})))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~(((\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM})))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~(((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}))\circ(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM}))))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~(((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}))-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM})))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(1)^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\circ\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[((((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(1)^{XM}))\circ((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\rho^{XM})))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Tr[(((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(1)^{XM}\circ\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}})\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\Pi(1)^{XM}\circ\rho^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\Pi(1)^{XM}\circ\rho^{XM}\|_{\infty}\cdot\Tr[\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}]\;\leq\;\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}||F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Above, we used the fact that

\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{Y}}\geq\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{X}\hat{Y}M}

in the third equality,

\displaystyle((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}))\circ(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{X}M}-((T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM})))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM})-(T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{XM}-\Pi(1)^{XM})

in the sixth equality,

T^{X\rightarrow\hat{X}}_{\epsilon^{1/4}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{M}
is
an isometry in the tenth equality.
The proof of the lemma is now complete.

\square

Lemma 22

\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{\hat{Y}M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{\hat{Y}M}]\leq\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|\beta^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})}}{|F^{\prime}_{\beta}||F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

Similar to proof of Lemma
21
above.

\square

Lemma 23

\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{M}]\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

Using Equations
69
and
16
, we get

\Tr[\hat{\rho}^{M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{M}]=\Tr[\rho^{M}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{M}]\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\Tr[\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{F^{\prime}_{\rho}}\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{M}]\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\Tr[\hat{\hat{\rho}}^{M}]\leq\frac{1+\sqrt{\epsilon}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.

\square

We now prove the following lemma which completes the simpler proof
of Lemma
7
by finally
showing the validity of Equation
74
.

Lemma 24

Suppose

\displaystyle\log A

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log\epsilon^{-1/20},

\displaystyle\log B

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{YM}\|\beta^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log\epsilon^{-1/20},

\displaystyle\log A+\log B

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle D^{\epsilon}_{\infty}(\rho^{XYM}\|\alpha^{X}\otimes\beta^{Y}\otimes\rho^{M})+\log\epsilon^{-1/20}.

Then for sufficiently small
\epsilon
,

\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]<\frac{1+\epsilon^{1/16}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}.

Proof:

We follow the proof method of Lemma
13
above,
but use
Lemmas
20
,
21
,

22
,
23

instead of
Lemmas
9
,
10
,

11
,
12
.
We get

\Tr[\hat{\sigma}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}\hat{\hat{\sigma}}^{\hat{X}^{A}\hat{Y}^{B}M}]<\frac{1+40\epsilon^{1/20}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}<\frac{1+\epsilon^{1/16}}{|F^{\prime}_{\alpha}|^{A}|F^{\prime}_{\beta}|^{B}|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}

for sufficiently small
\epsilon
.
This finishes the proof of the lemma.

\square

The proof of
Lemma
7
is now complete.

8
Fully smooth multipartite decoupling

In this section, we prove a fully smooth multipartite decoupling
theorem where we have to enlarge the Hilbert space dimensions of the
senders by a factor of
2
. Our proof is inspired by the simpler
proof of the fully smooth multipartite convex split lemma in
Section
7
.

The EPR state is defined as

\Phi^{A^{\prime}A}:=|A|^{-1}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=1}^{|A|}|a_{1},a_{1}\rangle^{A^{\prime}A}\langle a_{2},a_{2}|,

where
A^{\prime}
is another Hilbert space of the same dimension as
A
.
We remark that though Theorem
3
is stated in terms
of expectation over the Haar measure on unitaries as is the convention,
it continues to hold without change for expectation over a perfect
2-design of unitaries, and with minor additive terms for expectation
over an approximate 2-design of unitaries.

Theorem 3

Let
\mathcal{T}^{A_{1}A_{2}\rightarrow E}
be a completely positive trace
non increasing superoperator with Choi state given by

\tau^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}:=(\mathcal{T}^{A_{1}A_{2}\rightarrow E}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}})(\Phi^{A^{\prime}_{1}A_{1}}\otimes\Phi^{A^{\prime}_{2}A_{2}}).
.
Let
\rho^{A_{1}A_{2}R}
be a subnormalised density matrix.
Define
\hat{A}_{1}:=A_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Similarly define
\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}
,

\hat{A}_{2}
,
\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}
.
Define

\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R}:=\rho^{A_{1}A_{2}R}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}},

\tau^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}:=\tau^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}}.

Let
\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}
be the completely positive
superoperator corresponding to the inverse Choi image of

\tau^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}
. Then
\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}
is
the completely positive superoperator corresponding to projecting
from
\hat{A}_{1}\otimes\hat{A}_{2}
onto

(A_{1}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}})\otimes(A_{2}\otimes|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\mathbb{C}^{2}})

followed by
applying
4\mathcal{T}^{A_{1}A_{2}\rightarrow E}
.

Let
U^{\hat{A}_{1}}
,
U^{\hat{A}_{2}}
be unitary matrices on their respective
Hilbert spaces. Let
0<\epsilon<1
. Then,

\E_{U^{\hat{A}_{1}}U^{\hat{A}_{2}}}[\|(\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})((U^{\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes U^{\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{R})\circ\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R})-\tau^{E}\otimes\rho^{R}\|_{1}]\leq 18\epsilon^{1/128}

if

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{1}|R)_{\rho}+H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{1}|E)_{\tau}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{2}|R)_{\rho}+H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{2}|E)_{\tau}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{1}A_{2}|R)_{\rho}+H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}|E)_{\tau}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\log\epsilon^{-1},

\displaystyle|A_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-1},~~|A_{2}|^{2}>\epsilon^{-1},

where the expectation on the left is taken over independent choices
of unitaries
U^{\hat{A}_{1}}
,
U^{\hat{A}_{2}}
from their respective Haar measures.

We now proceed with the constructions required to prove
Theorem
3
.
Observe without loss of generality that
\rho^{A_{1}A_{2}R}
can be
assumed to be a normalised quantum state. Let
L_{R}
and
L_{E}
be the
ancilla Hilbert spaces required for flattening
\rho^{R}
and
\tau^{E}

respectively. By flattening using the CPTP superoperators

\mathcal{F}_{\rho}^{R\rightarrow L_{R}R}
,

\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}
, and by applying
Proposition
3
we observe that it suffices to show

\displaystyle\E_{U^{\hat{A}_{1}}U^{\hat{A}_{2}}}[\|((\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\rho}^{R\rightarrow L_{R}R})((U^{\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes U^{\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{R})\circ\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~{}-\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}(\tau^{E})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\rho}^{R\rightarrow L_{R}R}(\rho^{R})\|_{1}]\leq 81\epsilon^{1/64}.

Henceforth, we shall redefine

\displaystyle L_{R}R

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle R,

\displaystyle L_{E}E

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle E,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E},

\displaystyle(\mathbb{I}^{\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{\rho}^{R\rightarrow L_{R}R})(\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R},

\displaystyle((\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}\cdot\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E})\otimes\mathbb{I}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}})(\Phi^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes\Phi^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}\hat{A}_{2}})=(\mathcal{F}_{\tau}^{E\rightarrow L_{E}E}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}})(\tau^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\tau^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}.

Thus with this changed notation we will show

\E_{U^{\hat{A}_{1}}U^{\hat{A}_{2}}}[\|(\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})((U^{\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes U^{\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{R})\circ\rho^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R})-\tau^{E}\otimes\rho^{R}\|_{1}]\leq 81\epsilon^{1/64}.

(75)

Note that from its definition,

\mathcal{T}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}\rightarrow E}
increases the trace by at most a
multiplicative factor of
4
.

Define the tilting map

T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{1}}:|a_{1}\rangle^{A_{1}}\mapsto\sqrt{1-\epsilon^{1/4}}|a_{1}\rangle^{A_{1}}|0\rangle+\epsilon^{1/8}|a_{1}\rangle^{A_{1}}|1\rangle.

Define
T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{2}}
,

T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}}
,

T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}}
similarly.
Let
\Pi(1)^{A_{1}R}
,
\Pi(2)^{A_{2}R}
,
\Pi(3)^{A_{1}A_{2}R}
,

\Pi(1)^{A^{\prime}_{1}E}
,
\Pi(2)^{A^{\prime}_{2}E}
,
\Pi(3)^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}
be the
projectors promised by Proposition
2
achieving
the respective smooth conditional min entropy quantities for the
flattened states. More precisely, Propositions
3
,

2
guarantee, when the flattening parameter

\delta
is small enough, that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\Pi(1)^{A_{1}R}\circ\rho^{A_{1}R}&\leq&\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{1}|R)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{1}F^{\prime}_{\rho}},~~\Pi(2)^{A_{2}R}\circ\rho^{A_{2}R}\leq\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{2}|R)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{2}F^{\prime}_{\rho}},\\
\Pi(3)^{A_{1}A_{2}R}\circ\rho^{A_{1}A_{2}R}&\leq&\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A_{1}A_{2}|R)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{1}A_{2}F^{\prime}_{\rho}},\\
\Pi(1)^{A^{\prime}_{1}E}\circ\rho^{A^{\prime}_{1}E}&\leq&\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{1}|E)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A^{\prime}_{1}F^{\prime}_{\rho}},~~\Pi(2)^{A^{\prime}_{2}E}\circ\rho^{A^{\prime}_{2}E}\leq\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{2}|E)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A^{\prime}_{2}F^{\prime}_{\rho}},\\
\Pi(3)^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}\circ\rho^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}&\leq&\frac{(1+3\sqrt{\epsilon})2^{-H_{\mathrm{min}}^{\epsilon}(A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}|E)_{\rho}}}{|F^{\prime}_{\rho}|}\,\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}F^{\prime}_{\rho}}.\end{array}

(76)

Define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\hat{\rho}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R}&:=&(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})(\rho^{A_{1}A_{2}R}),\\
\hat{\tau}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}&:=&(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{E})(\tau^{A^{\prime}_{1}A^{\prime}_{2}E}),\\
\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R}&:=&\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}_{1}\hat{A}_{2}R}\\
&&{}-\spanning\{((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{1}R}-\Pi(1)^{A_{1}R}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}_{2}},\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{2}R}-\Pi(2)^{A_{2}R}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}_{1}},\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~(T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{1}}\otimes T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{R})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A_{1}A_{2}R}-\Pi(3)^{A_{1}A_{2}R})\},\\
\hat{\Pi}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}&:=&\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}E}\\
&&{}-\spanning\{((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{E})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A^{\prime}_{1}E}-\Pi(1)^{A^{\prime}_{1}E}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}},\\
&&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~((T_{\epsilon^{1/4}}^{A^{\prime}_{2}\rightarrow\hat{A}^{\prime}_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{I}^{E})(\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{A^{\prime}_{2}E}-\Pi(2)^{A^{\prime}_{2}E}))\otimes\hbox{\small 1\kern-3.8pt\normalsize 1}^{\hat{A}^{\prime}_{1}},\\
